## Measurement basis

Each case runs in the mode upstream registers it in (singlefile via `testgen_singlefile`, otherwise split: `onnx2c` + `testgen`), so results are directly comparable with upstream CTest.

| Measurement | Value |

| Upstream tests discovered (test/CMakeLists.txt) | 582 active, 69 commented out |

| Active upstream registrations missing from corpus | 0 |
| Reference tolerance | per-test from test/CMakeLists.txt |
| Cross-check (alternate mode re-run) | 1615 run, 1310 agree, 305 mode-sensitive |

### Mode-sensitive cases

These cases produce different outcomes in the two test modes. Upstream registers a test in whichever mode it passes (test/CMakeLists.txt); the metric uses that registered mode, and the divergence is recorded here rather than hidden. A common cause is the split harness embedding input constants with only six significant digits, while testgen_singlefile uses full precision and makes inputs compile-time constants (upstream note: "Accuracy breaks if not run in _singlefile").

## Provenance

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| onnx2c commit | 271d0e55e2c1699226d9bb9876f2769cef65f446 (dirty working tree) |
| ONNX version | v1.19.0 (submodule 57b9c6a4f) |
| Compiler | cc (Ubuntu 13.3.0-6ubuntu2~24.04.1) 13.3.0 |
| Reference tolerance | per-test from test/CMakeLists.txt (default 2e-05) |
| Campaign mode | exhaustive (complete: True, infrastructure clean: False) |
| Host | staff02.eda.cit.tum.de |
| Specification denominator | 198 operators in ai.onnx; other domains: ai.onnx=198, ai.onnx.ml=19, ai.onnx.preview.training=4 |

## Notes and limitations

- Operators that only appear in multi-operator graphs are counted for each operator in the graph; the solo_cases/solo_passed fields in the JSON report give the strict single-operator view.
- A registered operator proves dispatch exists, not that it is correct; only passing backend tests count as verified.
- Each case runs in the mode test/CMakeLists.txt registers it in; tests upstream does not register run in split mode (the onnx2c command-line path). Cases whose result differs in the other mode are listed under Mode-sensitive cases.
- Infrastructure errors (harness/tooling limitations) are reported separately and never counted as passing tests; a campaign can be complete while not being infrastructure clean.
